# Language-specific rules

Every rule that applies to some languages only, where it lives, the tests that show it works for its language
(positive) and the tests that show it does not fire for the others (negative). Collected 2026-10-19 on
`languages/ocr-engines` (67daf02) from the code, the plan `plans/2026-10-10-languages-implementation.md`,
`test-model.md` (LK1–LK27, AUD8–AUD12, OCR10, POP23), `yomitan-behavior.md` and `languages.md`.

When a language gets a new rule, add its row here, a positive test, and a negative case in
`LanguageRulesIsolationTest` (or next to the rule when it lives outside `core:common`).

Look-alike fixes and look-alike matching are for OCR results only, in every language (owner, 2026-10-20, plan
request 69): text read from apps is taken as it is. One-for-one fixes go in `LanguageSupport.ocrWord` or `ocrLines`,
which run only from `OcrPage.inLanguage` in `CompositeOcr`; `OverlayController.readAppText` skips them. Lookup
variants for OCR misreadings that cannot be fixed one for one (Welsh ý as ŷ, Hungarian ö as ő) run only for words
from OCR, which needs each line's source to reach the lookup (planned in groups 5b and 5c). Where people often type
the text itself that way (Uzbek o and g for oʻ and gʻ), the matching applies to any text. So does the planned
fallback of group 5b: a Welsh or Scottish Gaelic word without marks that finds no entry also tries the headwords that
differ from it only by marks (ty finds tŷ, bith finds bìth); Irish words are looked up as written.

## Paths and test names

| Short | Path |
|---|---|
| `lang/` | `core/common/src/main/java/com/vpr/screenlate/core/common/language/` |
| `api/` | `dictionary/api/src/main/java/com/vpr/screenlate/dictionary/api/` |
| `glue` | `scripts/yomitan-language/glue.js` (built into `dictionary/engine-hoshidicts/src/main/assets/yomitan-language/<iso>.js`) |
| `popup/` | `overlay/src/main/assets/popup/` |
| `render/` | `dictionary/render-yomitan/src/main/assets/yomitan-render/` |
| `overlay/` | `overlay/src/main/java/com/vpr/screenlate/overlay/` |
| `ocr/` | `core/ocr/src/main/java/com/vpr/screenlate/core/ocr/` |
| `Sentence.kt` | `core/anki/src/main/java/com/vpr/screenlate/core/anki/note/Sentence.kt` |

Tests: `Isolation` is `LanguageRulesIsolationTest` (core:common), `<Lang>Test` is `<Lang>SupportTest`, `glue` is
`scripts/yomitan-language/glue.test.mjs` (`node --test scripts/yomitan-language/glue.test.mjs`), `reading.mjs`, `note.mjs`,
`popup.mjs`, `definition.mjs` are the page tests in `overlay/src/test/js`, `I:` marks an instrumented test.

Status: **ok** (works, both kinds of tests), **fixed** (a bug this review fixed, with a test), **limit** (known, left
as it is; see below), **ask** (a behavior choice for the owner).

## Lookup pipeline (`dictionary:api`)

| Language | Rule | Code | Positive tests | Negative tests | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| de, nl, sv, no, da | A compound without an entry is split into the fewest words, then the split whose shortest part is longest (stads + bestuurder, not stadsbestuur + der), then the longest first, a linking s/es/n (Dutch s/en/e, Swedish s/e/u/o/a, Norwegian and Danish s/e) skipped; the page names the parts above their entries ("Compound: A + B"). A Swedish part shortened before its link (kyrko- of kyrka) is not tried: on wordfreq's top 50,000 it added mostly noise (plan 5a) | `api/Compounds.kt:12`, `api/DictionaryLookup.kt:92`, `GermanSupport.kt:21`, `DutchSupport.kt:24`, `SwedishSupport.kt` `compoundLinks`, `NorwegianSupport.kt` `compoundLinks`, `DanishSupport.kt` `compoundLinks` | CompoundsTest (`swedish links are skipped the same way…`: statsminister, skattepengar, barnabarn; `norwegian links are s and e`: arbeidsplass, fylkesmann, barnehage; `danish links are s and e`: statsminister, skolebørn, sportschef) | Isolation (`compoundLinks` only de, nl, sv, no, da), CompoundsTest (the lookup limit; kyrkogård not split) | fixed |
| de | A compound part's capitalized headwords (nouns) come first | `api/Compounds.kt:30`, `api/DictionaryLookup.kt:77` | CompoundsTest | Isolation (`capitalizedNouns` only de) | ok |
| fi | A compound without an entry is split as German's, with no linking element (`compoundLinks` empty): a genitive first part is found as the form row it is (kirjastonhoitaja: kirjaston + hoitaja; rautatieasema: rautatie + asema); the clitic and possessive rules and the form rows come first, as the split runs only when nothing covers the whole word | `lang/FinnishSupport.kt` `compoundLinks`; `api/Compounds.kt` | CompoundsTest `finnish joins its words without a link, a genitive first part is a word of its own` | Isolation `only German, Dutch, Swedish, Norwegian, Danish and Finnish split compounds…` | ok |

## Engine (`dictionary:engine-hoshidicts`)

| Language | Rule | Code | Positive tests | Negative tests | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ja | Native hoshidicts lookup with its deinflector; other languages through their Yomitan bundle in QuickJS | `HoshidictsEngine.kt:101` | I: HoshidictsEngineTest, LookupPipelineTest, LanguageLookupTest | — (one path per language) | ok |
| all but ja | Form-of entries are followed, a second form-of step allowed (шокирующее → шокировать), never back to the text it started from (Jansen → Janssen → Jansen), nor back to the scanned text after a Yomitan rule (книга as the plural of книго, the vocative of книга) | `cpp/language/candidate_lookup.cpp:39`, `:185`, `:199` | I: LanguageLookupTest (`formStepsDoNotLeadBackToTheText`, `ukrainian…`), HoshidictsEngineTest | language-agnostic by design | fixed |
| all but ja | A form whose lemma is the headword the text is itself adds no chain: the text's own entry with no rule applied, or a headword the text spells without its marks (kuca for kuća). | sq | Our endings are fallback rules: the engine looks up a text they give only when the scanned text found nothing through a headword or Yomitan's rules, one rule at a time by rank (the longest ending first; at equal length nouns, adjectives, then verbs: lejen → leje before lej), until one reaches an entry; each is undone once and alone and names the part of speech it must reach, and a noun ending that leaves a case ending reaches only a headword of that text, not a form-of entry (partneritet is not partnerit + -et). On the three Wikipedia samples of the plan they find 613, 603 and 639 tokens (3.6-3.7% of the words), as measured, 1.3-1.4% of their hits wrong | `glue.js` `fallbackRules`, `fallbackStep`; `cpp/language/candidate_lookup.cpp` (`p`, `l`) | glue `albanian endings give texts with their rank, undone once and alone…`, `an albanian noun ending that leaves a case ending reaches only a headword of that text`; I: LanguageLookupTest `albanianEndingsFindOnlyWordsWithoutAnEntryTheFirstEndingThatReachesOne` | every other LanguageLookupTest (no text carries a rank, so all are looked up at once as before) | ok |
| sw | No Yomitan descriptor: the bundle takes Czech's (capitalization) under `sw`, with Ukrainian's apostrophe variants (ng'ombe, ng’ombe) and 21 verb prefix rules of our own (`swahiliTransforms`): the subject persons and noun classes, the negative ha- and si-, the tenses -li- -na- -ta- -me- -nge- -ngali- -ki- -ka-, the negative -ku- and -ja-, hu-, the relative -ye- -cho- -o- … after a tense and as a suffix without one (waishio, iliyo), the objects, the reflexive -ji- and the infinitive ku-, taken off from the outside in; the verb is looked up as written, with ku- and with a hyphen (soma, kusoma, -soma). The stem keeps two letters, and two syllables after -li-, -na-, -ta-, -taka-, -me-, -nge-, -ngali- and a relative after a tense, where one-syllable verbs keep their ku- (alikula → kula; Angela is no a-nge-la, Pamela no pa-me-la); after -ki-, -ka-, the negative -ku-, -ja-, hu-, an object and ku- one syllable is enough (akiwa, akaja, hakuwa, kumpa). The rules are named in words, which the generator writes as labels (`inflections_sw.xml`, 42 strings, in all 14 locales) | `sw.js` bundle; `own-transforms.js` `swahiliTransforms`, `swahiliTense`, `swahiliStem`; `build.mjs`; `generate-inflections.mjs` (`hasLabel`); `res/values*/inflections_sw.xml` | glue `swahili verb prefixes come off from the outside in…`, `one-syllable swahili verbs keep their ku-…`, `every swahili rule has a name and a description of its own…`, `swahili ng' is tried with every apostrophe`; I: LanguageLookupTest `swahiliPrefixesComeOffFromTheOutsideInAndReachVerbsOnlyWhereTheTextHasNoEntry` | glue `swahili prefixes stay out of other languages` | ok |
| sw | The texts between the steps carry the private-use marker and are not looked up (`markedLanguages`); every text the prefixes reach carries the tag `v`, which keeps only glossaries tagged v or non-lemma (`taggedRules`: aliunga reaches unga "to join", not "flour"; wali, rice, is no wa-li- form); a result reached only through the prefixes is dropped where the same text finds a result without them (`inflectionRulesOnlyWithoutEntry`: kupa is not ku- + pa). On Leipzig's Swahili Wikipedia (1,427,746 tokens) sw-en finds 81.4% as written and the prefixes 8.9% more, 9.7% missing; with every Swahili dictionary 83.6% and 7.5%. | kk, ky | Noun chains do not reach the pronouns whose case forms change their stem: Kyrgyz ал, бул, ошол, Kazakh ол, бұл (and бул, as OCR writes it), сол; a word list, not wty's `pron` tag. Checked on Leipzig's 30,000 news sentences: the rules alone reach 7.8% of the tokens (28,244 of 363,084, 8,347 words); of 40 frequency-weighted ones, 37 reach the right headword first (the misses: çalındı to çalın, döyüşən to döyüşə, təsadüfi to təsadüf) | `scripts/yomitan-language/own-transforms.js` `azerbaijaniTransforms`, `turkicTransforms`; `build.mjs` `addedTransforms` | glue `azerbaijani noun endings come off in the order of the grammar…`, `azerbaijani verb forms reach the infinitive…`; I: LanguageLookupTest `azerbaijaniFindsItsWordsThroughEndingsWithTheTurkishCasePairsAndTriesSchwaAfterOcr` | glue (kitabya, kitabna, suyu reach nothing; gəldi no gəlmaq), `azerbaijani rules stay out of other languages` | ok |

## Recognition and app text (`core:ocr`, overlay)

| Language | Rule | Code | Positive tests | Negative tests | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| every language | The docked bubble shows the first letter of the language's own name, capitalized (Deutsch D, русский Р, čeština Č, العربية ع after the article, فارسی ف); a script the language has to itself keeps its most typical character (あ, 中, 한, Ω, ქ, Հ, ท, ខ, বা, हि, א). When another quick language's glyph has the same shape, both show their codes: shapes compare without marks (Č as C) and with Cyrillic and Greek letters that look Latin as those (Р as P, С as C, К as K). Cyrillic-script languages write their codes in Cyrillic (РУ, УК, БЕ, КК, КЫ, СР), on the bubble and in the e-ink codes; a language added later sets its glyph and, for Cyrillic, its `codeLabel` (owner, 2026-10-28, replacing the hand-picked ß, Ř, Æ, Ў) | `language/BubbleGlyph.kt` `bubbleGlyph`, `glyphShape`; `LanguageSupport.glyph`, `codeLabel`; `OverlayController.showGlyph` | BubbleGlyphTest (EN/ES, РУ/PL, СР/CY, КЫ/SW, CS/CY, a photo's language) | BubbleGlyphTest `different letters keep the glyphs` (Қ and К, ع and ف) | ok |
| each OCR script | ML Kit model by script, Japanese and Chinese apart, Devanagari bundled and used for Bengali too; Armenian, Cyrillic, Georgian, Greek, Khmer and Thai have none | `ocr/mlkit/MlKitOcrEngine.kt` `model`, `options` | MlKitOcrEngineTest (Hindi and Bengali are read) | MlKitOcrEngineTest (only the languages of scripts without a model are not read) | ok |

| th | Tesseract gives Thai one "word" per letter or cluster; they are joined with no separator, and with a space where the gap exceeds 0.4 of the line height (measured on the th-page screens: spaces 0.28-0.74, gaps inside words up to 0.37), and at a line end with none | `ocr/tesseract/TesseractOcrEngine.kt` `tesseractPage`; `ThaiSupport.kt` `ocrSpaceGap` | TesseractPageTest `thai clusters…` | Isolation (`ocrSpaceGap` null elsewhere), TesseractPageTest (other languages join by their separator) | ok |
| km | No ML Kit or Paddle model; Tesseract `khm`, the fast model recommended (1.4 MB against best's 8.1 MB; about 9% errors on clean screens, 57% on hard ones). Tesseract reads Khmer phrase by phrase, so a gap wider than 0.15 of the line height is a space (checked on the emulator: the spaces of a rendered paragraph kept, none added inside words). | cy | ML Kit's Latin model main (2.19% of characters wrong over the four test screens: ŷ as ý in 6 of 8, ŵ as w in 3 of 4); Tesseract fast and best (0.00%, every ŵ ŷ read) and Paddle v6 and Latin v5 (1.09%, 1.25%) all marked "more accurate" (`MORE_ACCURATE`), unticked at setup; detectors listed apart | `scripts/ocr-models/tessdata_catalog.py`, `paddle_models.py` `MORE_ACCURATE`; catalog `tesseract-fast-cym`, `tesseract-best-cym`, `paddle-v6-cy`, `paddle-latin-cy` | CatalogFileTest `welsh offers three wiktionaries and its transcription…`; test_tessdata_catalog, test_paddle_models (`MORE_ACCURATE`) | test_paddle_models (one mark per other language) | ok |
| ga | ML Kit's Latin model main (0.45% of characters wrong over the four test screens); Tesseract `gle` fast and best (0.37%, 0.45%) and Paddle v6 and Latin v5 (0.07%, 0.00%) listed unticked, none marked: less than 0.5 points to gain (rows "OCR engine choice", "Diacritics of Latin-script languages"); detectors listed apart | `scripts/ocr-models/tessdata_catalog.py` `LANGUAGES` ga → gle, `paddle_models.py` `LATIN`; catalog `tesseract-fast-gle`, `tesseract-best-gle`, `paddle-v6-ga`, `paddle-latin-ga` | CatalogFileTest `irish offers four wiktionaries and its transcription…`; test_tessdata_catalog, test_paddle_models (no mark for ga) | — | ok |

## Bugs fixed in this review

1. French and Italian took the n of rock’n’roll for an elided word, so a word aimed after it started at "roll".
2. Turkish "ŞTİ." in capitals missed its abbreviation (root lowercase gives i with a combining dot).
3. "３．１４" ended a Japanese or Chinese sentence at the full-width period.
4. Yomitan's word resolution cuts a word at a combining mark (`[^\p{Letter}]` in `_getNextSubstring`): stressed Russian
   text typed with U+0301 also showed entries for "ко", NFD Vietnamese for "hoa". The lookup now drops such matches in
   languages looked up by words.
5. `{sentence}` took in every earlier sentence of the paragraph when a closing quote had no opening one (“ja” in
   German, Russian or Latvian text, where “ closes „) or a list item's ")" stood before the word; forward, a „…“ in
   Polish text ran the sentence to the paragraph end.
6. App text joined the last word of a line and the first of the next without a space when the wrap or line break had
   no character location ("Helloworld"), so in spaced languages the aimed word or phrase across a wrap was wrong.
7. `{sentence-furigana}` stepped one letter after unknown text and found words inside words (happy in unhappy).
8. German "Plan B." and "Vitamin A." did not end a sentence (single letters were listed as abbreviations alone), and
   "o. Ä." did end one at "Ä." (ä was missing).
9. Polish "Jest ok. Idziemy." did not end at "ok.".
10. English quotes in German text: 'Er sagte “Komm.” Dann…' gave the next sentence a leading ” and left it out of
    the first.
11. Structured content data keys starting with Hangul, Cyrillic, a digit or another non-Latin character got
    `data-sc-`, unlike Yomitan, so a dictionary's CSS written for Yomitan missed them; keys Yomitan skips (foo-bar)
    were kept.
12. The frequency chip's initial ignored Turkish case rules (I for ingilizce instead of İ).
13. App text dropped a combining mark typed apart, whose box a text view reports empty, and ended the line there:
    hoà written with U+0300 was looked up as "hoa" (the flower only), and ко́шка was highlighted as two overlapping
    boxes. Found on the emulator (LK28); the language engine itself handles such text (glue test).
14. Lens joins the halves of a word hyphenated at a line end with no separator ("impor-tant"), so the break rules
    never matched and neither half found anything (LK30).

Found while adding Serbo-Croatian:

15. Yomitan writes a warning through `console` when a text processor gives more than 4096 variants. QuickJS has no
    console, so the warning threw and the whole lookup failed. Serbo-Croatian Latin text with many c, s and z
    letters reaches the cut ("cecescecescecesc"). Node's `vm` contexts always have a console, so the glue tests
    missed it; the timing check on the emulator found it.
16. With the system in Croatian, Serbian or Bosnian, the sentence translation of Serbo-Croatian text defaulted to that
    language, which only repeats the text.
17. Yomitan's Serbo-Croatian accent processor drops the marks of readings from Latin vowels only: Cyrillic text
    written with them (ку̏ћа, as in textbooks and Wiktionary) found nothing.

Found while adding Ukrainian and Belarusian:

18. Yomitan's Ukrainian "nominative plural" takes книга for the plural of книго, and Wiktionary lists книго as the
    vocative of книга, so a feminine noun in -а typed or read in its dictionary form showed "nominative plural «
    vocative singular" beside it. The engine drops a form-of step back to the scanned text through another text.
19. OCR reads a word of one letter in Latin as often as in Cyrillic (і, у, В, а): no other letter shows its script,
    and the look-alike fix needed a Cyrillic letter in the word, so such a word found nothing, in Russian too.

Found while adding Greek:

20. The Lingua Libre search asked for the language's ISO 639-3 code after its Wikidata item (`LL-Q9129 (ell)-…`).
    Greek recordings carry `ell` (167), `gre` (130) or `mis` (84), so more than half were never found (μητέρα has one
    under `gre` only). The search now takes any three-letter code after the item, for every language; the other
    languages checked have one code each (fra, deu, nld, ces, msa, cmn, srp, bel, tgl), with 105 more French files and
    9 more Mandarin ones under other codes.

Found while splitting Croatian and Serbian:

21. Cloud recognition reads Serbian Јој (all three letters look Latin) as the Latin "Joj"; the look-alike fix needs a
    Cyrillic letter in the word, so the Cyrillic text showed the Latin entry joj. On a Cyrillic line such words are
    now written in Cyrillic (found on the emulator).
22. The Commons search for `Sr-вода` also matched `LL-Q7737 (rus)-Tatiana Kerbush-вода.wav`: its regular expression
    is not anchored to the title's start. Wiktionary files now count only when the whole title matches.

Found while adding Hindi:

23. Only non-spacing marks counted as part of a word. Devanagari vowel signs such as ि and ा are spacing marks, so a
    word aimed after one started there (कि|ताब looked up from ताब), and the word before the period of "डॉ."
    came out empty, so no abbreviation matched. Spacing and enclosing marks and the zero-width joiners now belong to the word in every spaced
    language.

Fixed afterwards from the limits below (2026-10-19, the owner asked which are worth fixing):

23. ML Kit leaves a line-final hyphen out of its words ("impor" / "tant"), so a hyphenated word was never joined with
    it. Its line text keeps the hyphen (checked on the emulator, German), so the hyphen is put back after the word.
24. Czech Roman ordinals ("Karel IV. byl") ended the sentence at their period. A Roman numeral in capitals before a
    lowercase word is now an ordinal in every ordinal language (cs, de, pl, lv, tr, hr, sr).
25. Compounds split greedily, the longest word first: Dutch stadsbestuurder gave stadsbestuur + der. The split now
    takes the fewest parts, then the longest shortest part (stads + bestuurder).
26. `{phonetic-transcriptions}` left out the transcriptions' tags, which Yomitan puts in italics before each one.

Found while adding Bengali:

27. Wiktionary exports write some form rows with leftovers of their templates, keyed under the whole text and so
    never found: in wty bn-en 5,972 of 53,646 form rows have a romanization after the form ("মাসটি (maśṭa
    (colloquial), maśṭi (formal))", "কলকাতাকে  / kolkatake"), 337 an ending set apart (মুখ ে; 90 such rows in
    hi-en), 78 an unfilled template ({{{3}}}ে); hy-en has 1,883 of 942,487 with a romanization or two spellings
    (ծագմանը / ծագմանն), ka-en 11, and 73 unfilled templates. They are now keyed by their words.

Found while adding Georgian:

28. Yomitan's Georgian descriptor files its rules under `kat`, not `ka`, and the glue looked rules up by the language
    code, so none loaded (Yomitan itself never applies them either). The glue now takes each language's rules by
    the descriptor's iso; the inflection generator takes the transforms in the language's folder, whatever code
    they name.
29. A rule that strips a whole word (Georgian -მა from მა) left an empty text, which was queried; the glue drops
    empty candidates in every language (glue `georgian rules load…`).

Found while adding Thai:

30. A system font whose `wght` axis does not reach the normal weight (Android 15's Noto Sans Khmer reports 26-190)
    was declared with that range, below the 400 the page asks for; such a font is now declared at 400
    (FontFilesTest).

Found while adding Khmer:

31. ICU joins some Khmer phrases into one word (ក្រុងភ្នំពេញ, នៅផ្ទះ, រាល់ថ្ងៃ), which dictionaries have only in
    parts; the lookup started at the joined word's start, so an aim at ពេញ showed ក្រុង (city). It now also starts
    inside the aimed word when nothing found reaches the aimed character, for Thai as well (EarlierStartsTest,
    KhmerSupportTest; checked on the emulator, LK47).

Found while adding Arabic:

32. A Wikimedia Commons error answer (HTTP 200 with `error`, such as too many regular expression searches at once)
    counted as "no recording" and was cached, so the word stayed without audio until the cache expired. It now
    fails the source (AudioPagesTest, AudioFinderTest).
33. Inflection template names among a form row's tags (`ar-infl-a`, in every language's Wiktionary exports) were
    shown as steps of the inflection chain (LanguageLookupTest).
34. Tesseract word boxes could reach over the words before them (`ara` gives the first symbol of a vowelled word a
    box from the line's start), so the highlight covered several words (TesseractPageTest; found on the emulator,
    LK48).
35. In a language without audio regions, transcriptions were sorted as if each were of an unranked region instead of
    keeping the dictionary's order: Egyptian [keˈtæːb] came before /ki.taːb/ (popup.mjs; found on the emulator).

Found while adding Hebrew:

36. Transcriptions written `//a/ [b]/` (6% of the Hebrew ones, a few Arabic, Persian and Urdu ones) showed with their
    extra slashes as one transcription; they are now split (TranscriptionsTest; found on the emulator, LK50).
37. Cloud recognition gave a Hebrew line with a number, ₪ and a Latin word in a jumbled order, so the scan text after
    a word ran into words before it (היום iPhone 15 קניתי); such a line is now ordered by its word boxes
    (LensProtocolTest; found on the emulator, LK50).
38. Hebrew's `{reading}`, the word itself, had no `<span dir="rtl">` in the suggested field templates, though it is
    Hebrew text (FieldTemplateTest, HebrewSupportTest; found on the emulator, ANK19).
39. Aimed at a later part of a hyphenated word that has no entry of its own, the first part's results stayed after
    the whole word (бала-шағамен aimed at шағамен: бала-шаға, then бала); the whole word now shows alone
    (EarlierStartsTest; found on the emulator, LK52).
40. Kazakh and Kyrgyz accusative and genitive endings in д and т were taken after a vowel, so a past tense read as
    a noun with two endings (оқыды: оқ "arrow" with -ы and -ды; Kyrgyz окуду: ок); they now follow a consonant
    only (`/C`, the grammar's `consonants`; Kazakh у counts, as in суды) (glue tests; found on the emulator, LK51).

Found in the device checks of 2026-10-22:

41. The Czech "n. I." fix matched word by word, but cloud recognition gives "n", ".", "I", "." as four words and ML
    Kit "n.I." as one, so neither engine's reading was fixed on the device. `ocrLines` now matches over the line's
    words joined with spaces, and "n.l." and "př.n.l." are abbreviations (CzechSupportTest, LanguageRulesIsolationTest;
    ANK22).

Found while checking the Kazakh and Kyrgyz rules on data (2026-10-22, "Kazakh and Kyrgyz rules checked on data"):

42. A Kazakh word ending in у took no linking vowel, so a verbal noun or a noun in у with a possessive was not found
    (болуы, сайлауы, күйеуі, оқуым: 545 of Leipzig's 30,000 most frequent forms, 8,173 occurrences of 981,616); у now
    takes one as a consonant does (the grammar's `glides`; glue tests).
43. The manner and equative endings had no form with the possessive's н, so пікірінше, сөзінше, баласындай (Kazakh)
    and өзүнчө, көбүнчө, өзүндөй (Kyrgyz) were not found or only keyed through a wrong second-person reading;
    -нша -нше -ндай -ндей and the Kyrgyz -нча … -ндөй now follow a possessive's vowel, as -нда does (glue tests).
44. The Kazakh converb of a verb in п writes у for it (тап- → тауып, шап- → шауып: 330 occurrences); the converb
    now takes the п back (`уып>п`), so тауып finds табу (glue tests).

Found while rechecking against wty 2026.10.22 (2026-10-22):

45. Kazakh and Kyrgyz one-letter words showed the entry of the Arabic letter they stand for: Wiktionary links each
    Cyrillic letter to its Arabic equivalent with the tag `Cyrillic` (а → ا, в → ۋ; kk-en 73 rows, ky-en 18, both
    releases), which the filter for other scripts missed (Kyrgyz а 148 and в 794 occurrences in the 30,000 most
    frequent forms, Kazakh о 69). `Cyrillic` and `Cyrillic sometimes` joined `ignoredFormTags` (ShownResultsTest;
    test-model case LK63).

Found while building Slovak (2026-10-22):

46. In a language that keys every headword without its marks, a form-of item of a word with definitions was keyed as a
    plain form, while the word itself, keyed the same way, counted as respelled and came after it: robit showed urobiť
    and zrobiť (robiť is their imperfective) before robiť. Form rows keyed so are now respelled too (the form-of
    table's flag, version 2), so robit shows robiť first, as robiť does (LanguageLookupTest
    `slovakFindsWordsWrittenWithoutTheirMarksTheExactSpellingFirst`; test-model case LK64). This touches every language
    with `everyHeadword` (Greek, Romanian, Lithuanian, Arabic, Persian, Hindi, Khmer, Kazakh, Kyrgyz); dictionaries
    imported before keep the old order until they are imported again.

47. Turning Czech off with its files deleted, or "Delete all" on its card under "Turned off", while Slovak was on
    deleted the Czech definitions Slovak's lookups fall back to, although the card and the dialog had counted only
    Czech's frequency list and transcriptions. Czech's language code is its dictionaries' code, so the files' codes
    took in every Czech dictionary. The deletion now leaves out the dictionaries a language that stays on reads
    (`LanguageFiles` `dictionariesOf`, OcrModelsTest `czech keeps the definitions slovak falls back to while slovak
    stays on`; test-model case DIC34).

## Limits left as they are

- Bengali endings are undone once: ছেলেদেরকে finds ছেলেদের, not ছেলে. Verb forms come only from Wiktionary's form
  rows. The endings are tried on every word that ends like one (সাগর also queries সাগ); such a word shows only if a
  dictionary has it, after the full word.
- An ending also reaches the form rows under the word it leaves: বইটির shows বই first, then বু (of which বই is a
  spelling) and the verb বওয়া (of which বই is the first person present), as no part of speech rules them out.
- An ending set apart as য় (কলকাতা য়, 89 rows in wty bn-en) is not joined, since য় is a letter, not a mark; the -য়
  ending finds কলকাতা anyway, without the form's tag (locative).
- wty bn-pl writes romanizations as forms ("biṣ / bish" → বিষ); they are keyed as written, Latin text that Bengali
  text never matches.
- Georgian rules chain and are tried on every word that ends like an ending; with three letters left, some words
  also show a wrong neighbor after the right entry (არის, "is", also shows არი). Verbs come only from the form rows.
- Persian rules are tried on every word that ends or starts like an affix; with two letters left, 3,902 of the
  11,017 commonest words that are entries also show a neighbor after the right entry (است → اس, باید → اید), most
  through a one-letter enclitic (-ت, -م, -ش, -ی). Verbs come only from the form rows, and a form neither keyed nor
  reached through a prefix shows what its first part is (می‌تواند shows می).
- Hebrew prefixes are tried on every word that starts like one; with two characters left, 3,429 of the 10,911
  commonest words that are entries also show a neighbor after the right entry (היה → יה, כבר → בר). Verbs and
  noun forms come only from Wiktionary's form rows (no own rules for endings), and a pointed form the
  dictionary lacks is found only by its letters.
- Recognition of Hebrew: cloud recognition turns the maqaf into a word break, Tesseract into a hyphen, and Tesseract
  garbles Latin words and sometimes the gershayim (צה״ל as וצהייל).

- `Compounds.word` stops at a combining mark in NFD text; OCR and app text give NFC, so a German compound typed in
  NFD is not split. Not worth the mapping it would need.
- Furigana of languages without reading rules goes through Yomitan's Japanese distribution, as Yomitan does;
  `{part-of-speech}` uses Japanese names, as Yomitan does.
- `∥` example lines are dropped from definition copies in every language (`popup/definition.js:320`): by design,
  ∥ separates an example from its translation wherever it appears.
- reading-ru rules are for Russian only. Ukrainian and Belarusian need none: their dictionaries give lemmas no
  reading, and the forms' stress is dropped from the key at import.
- wty uk-ru has some words twice, under ' and ’ (п'ятниця, п’ятниця): both are found, as two entries that look
  the same. Only 3 of its 99,684 headwords are such pairs; merging them would need an apostrophe rule in the grouping
  of every language.
- Ukrainian "див." (see) is also the genitive plural of диво, and "пор." (compare) of пора (a pore); a sentence ending in
  either does not end there.
- Paddle lines are paragraphs of their own, so a word hyphenated at a line end in PaddleOCR text is looked up as two
  halves; Lens takes separators from its response. Joining every word across a line break without a hyphen would
  find wrong words ("the" / "rapist").
- The frequency chip's initial follows the page language, not the dictionary title's (unknown): an English title in
  a Turkish profile gets İ.
- A word hyphenated at a line end is joined only when the second half is in lowercase; a real hyphen with a capital
  after it (Max- Planck) is tried with the hyphen only. A hyphen at a line end in Japanese, Chinese or Korean text
  is left alone.
- Wiktionary's s-forms (werkgevers, dorps, stads) are words of their own, so a compound split mostly takes the
  linking s with the first part.
- The compound split (fewest parts, then the longest shortest part) errs where a linking s could start the next
  word: urlaub + stag for Urlaubstag, geschlecht + steil; adverbs in -erweise (glücklich + erweise) and numerals
  (fünf + zehnmal) split wrong too, but dictionaries usually have them whole. Checked on 20,000 long lemma headwords
  of wty de-en and nl-en each, the headword hidden from the lookup: the rule changes 2% (de) and 4% (nl) of the
  longest-first splits; of 80 changed ones 47 got better and 26 worse in German, about 50 and 13 in Dutch (the worse
  Dutch ones mostly derived words where both splits are wrong: aanvaar + ding, aanvaard + ing). A wordfreq tie-break
  among splits with as many parts was worse: short frequent words win (middag + ten, dan + school, pijl + taart).
- A Roman ordinal before a capital ("Ludwig XIV. König von Frankreich") ends the sentence, as a year does.
- A word that is also a form of itself (kuća, genitive plural of kuća) shows no form name, not even behind "+N": the
  popup no longer says that kuća is also the genitive plural (Yomitan shows it).
- Persian ordinals in -مین after نه lose to the negative prefix ن-: نهمین shows همین first, then نهم (همین is more
  frequent). Names in -تون and -مون that the dictionary lacks show a word through the ending (کلینتون, بوستون,
  پایتون, سالمون), as do the spoken همشون and خونمون (هم, خون instead of همه, خونه).
- Serbian text that mixes scripts within a line (a Latin name in a Cyrillic sentence) follows the line's majority:
  a Latin word made only of look-alikes there and not in capitals (Joe, Hey, copy, Oppo) becomes Cyrillic; other
  Latin words stay. The reverse: a Serbian word in capitals of look-alikes alone in running text (СССР read as CCCP)
  stays Latin. Both look the same on screen; only the copied text, the lookup and the translation differ.
- Serbian's model list has no ML Kit (the owner: Serbian is mostly Cyrillic; question 66): the Latin choice is
  PaddleOCR v6. Tesseract has srp only, as decided (no srp_latn).
- Greek π.Χ. (before Christ) lowercases to π.χ. (for example), so it never ends a sentence; μ.Χ. does.
- The Greek look-alike c → ς applies inside a word too, where a Latin c in Greek text is rare anyway.
- Only the tonos (U+0301) is dropped from Greek headwords: polytonic text (breathings, the grave and the circumflex)
  finds only headwords written the same way. Modern Greek dictionaries are monotonic.
- Greek text without tonos finds every headword that differs by it, as decided: ποτε shows ποτέ (never) and πότε
  (when). In wty el-en 7,921 tonos-free spellings stand for two or more headwords.

- Thai unit tests segment with ICU4J 66.1, the ICU of Android 11 (minSdk 30); newer phones have a newer ICU, which may
  cut some words otherwise (on the 4,466 example sentences 66.1 finds 90,328 matches from its word starts, 75.1 90,343). The device
  check (LK46) covers the emulator's segmentation.
- Thai spaces narrower than 0.4 of the line height are lost from Tesseract's text (the narrowest measured: 0.28); a
  space only matters there for the end of an Anki sentence.
- Thai headwords written with a space (63 in wty th-en, mostly proverbs: ยอมงอ ไม่ยอมหัก) are found only within the
  scan length, and the Anki sentence ends at their space.
- A Thai sentence ending in ๆ with a space after it runs on into the next one, as a space beside ๆ never ends a
  sentence (decided): on the emulator page the line ending in จริงๆ and the next line made one sentence, as cloud
  recognition put them in one paragraph and joined them with a space.
- Tesseract joins the lines of a Thai paragraph with nothing, so a sentence ending at a line end takes the next
  line's first word (…ของไทยใน); cloud recognition joins lines with a space.
- The Thai National Corpus counts the words of its own segmentation: compounds it splits (การตลาด, กรุงเทพมหานคร)
  carry no rank.

- Khmer unit tests segment with ICU4J 66.1 as Thai's do; the emulator's newer ICU joined the same phrases (LK47).
- A start inside the aimed word is tried only when nothing found reaches the aimed character, so a joined word that
  a dictionary has whole hides its parts' entries for a later character (they show when the aim is on the next
  word); the inner starts cost one lookup each until one covers the character (at most the word's clusters up to
  the aim).
- Cloud recognition gives Khmer and Thai word boxes only, split evenly into characters: an aim at the very edge of a
  word can land on its neighbour's share (on the emulator an aim at the left edge of a word gave the word before).
- Tesseract `khm` drops vowel signs, reads ត as ទ at times and breaks a line where it starts with ្រ or a stacked
  consonant; it joins the lines of a paragraph with nothing, as for Thai.
- Commons has few Khmer recordings (13 from Lingua Libre, 9 Wiktionary `Km-` files in October 2026): most words get
  their audio from the phone's text to speech (Google's Khmer voice spoke ទឹក on the emulator), after two searches
  that find nothing.
- Khmer has no frequency dictionary (wordfreq has none and no corpus list was chosen).
- Arabic homographs reached through form rows whose lemma is written without harakat: ذهبت shows ذَهَب (gold) before
  ذَهَبَ (to go), الكتب shows كَتَبَ (to write) before كِتَاب, whose plural it is.
  Yomitan does the same; an owner question (see the plan).
- Tesseract `ara` garbles Latin words inside Arabic lines and reads "50%" as 9650; PaddleOCR's Arabic model, which
  keeps them, waits for release with the other models.
- A sentence that really ends in a capital letter runs on into the next one when that starts with a capital
  (vitamin C. Then, World War I. The), since an initial before a capitalized word holds the sentence (group 4e
  question 19; Lithuanian news has 105 such ends in 200,000 sentences, against 9,048 initials).
- Verb forms of a verb that wty gives only as a noun or adjective are not found, as a verb chain keeps only the
  definitions tagged `v`: Kyrgyz жашаган, сурап, чакырды (жашоо, суроо, чакыруу are tagged `n`), Kazakh өзгертіп,
  күшейтіп; the owner keeps the filter (group 4e plan, question 20: it also drops about three times as many wrong
  readings in Kyrgyz).
- Kyrgyz nouns in -ыш that wty lacks are read as verbal nouns: of the 30,000 most frequent forms, 39 (about 1,270
  occurrences) get a wrong first reading (келишим "agreement" and its forms as келүү, талаш, жарыш, жылыш, катышы),
  and 20 forms of жумуш "work" (620) show жумуу after жумуш.
- Noun endings read first where another reading is right, on the 30,000 most frequent forms (group 4e plan,
  "Noun endings on Leipzig's top forms"): endings whose consonant does not fit the stem (айта, карата as айт with the
  dative), -сыз "without" as the polite second person (мыйзамсыз, белгисиз), verb forms read as a more frequent noun
  at equal chain length (Kazakh алды as ал "red" through the form row's tie, тапты, жатты), н read as ң (болатын →
  болат), and pronoun forms wty lacks (аны, андан, мындан); owner questions in that plan.
- Kazakh пен and бен (and, with) and the particles written apart (та, те, па, пе, ме) are not wty headwords, so the
  popup finds nothing for them (1,940 occurrences of пен in 1.1 million words).

- Romanian forms are stored without marks and a form row's spelling is in its reading, which the lookup does not
  compare with the text: casa (the definite of casă) also finds cașă through the form row casa (reading cașa), and
  the text "cașa" finds casă as well. Showing the forms whose reading matches the written text first would need the
  engine to return the form row's reading.

- Latin: M'. (Manius) is not held, as the apostrophe ends the word before the period. A word needing two u read as v
  at once (uiuere for vivere) is found only where the dictionary lists the u spelling. In v-spelled text a u before
  a vowel also finds the v word (voluit also shows volvo, after volo). A word with an enclitic that is listed itself
  (videsne, a form of viden) takes no enclitic result.

- Lithuanian: an initial before a capital (A. Veryga) holds the sentence, as in every spaced language since group
  4e's question 19 (9,048 initials before a capital in 200,000 sentences, 105 real ends, mostly °C.). A year, century or day abbreviation before a capital ends it too
  (2020 m. Lietuvoje), which is right where a new sentence starts and wrong before a place or a name. A word written
  without marks whose bare spelling is another word shows that word first (i before į, ruda before rūda), and some
  foreign words find a Lithuanian one (de → dė, as → aš). OCR text turns é in foreign names into ė (Josė). About 29%
  of lowercase words in running text are not in wty lt-en (tiek, nebuvo, būna).

- Slovak: text written without marks whose bare spelling is not in sk-en finds a marked word through the keys (tu →
  tú 2,921 times in 200,000 sentences, i → í, u → ú), and that own result keeps the Czech tu (here) out. pri and pred
  are in neither dictionary (Czech writes při, před). With Slovak's own dictionaries deleted and only Czech ones left,
  scanning says no dictionary is installed. A backup of Slovak takes Czech's frequency list and models as well
  (`lookupDictionaryCodes` names the whole code). A spaced "s. r. o." and initials (P. J. Šafárik) end the sentence
  before a capital, as in Czech. The "n. I." OCR fix stays Czech's.
- Slovenian: Wiktionary's Slovenian dictionaries lack many common words (ob, naj, sicer, saj, ter, zato, proti,
  glede, namreč, treba): 34.9% of the lowercase words of Leipzig's news are in neither sl-en nor sl-ru; FreeDict's
  slv-eng would find 3.4% more (question 27 of the group 5a plan). Text without the caron whose bare spelling is in
  neither dictionary finds a word with it (zdi → ždí 386 times in 200,000 sentences, krize → kríže, zame → žáme),
  0.07% of the words, besides the right ones (roza → roža, ze → že); the keys are the owner's decision "Words
  without their marks". 97 form-of items in sl-en name a lemma with ə or ł (várən, ọ̑gəł), which loses its marks but
  not those letters, so no headword matches it. Leipzig's news has è for č in 7,272 sentences (pages decoded with
  the wrong code page); such words find nothing. The i of "t. i." takes its period from any single letter beside it;
  before a capital, a single letter with a period is an initial and holds the sentence (n. I. Potem, Matej T.
  Vatovec), as in every spaced language.
- Esperanto: wty eo-en and eo-ru find 88.3% of the news's lowercase words and 89.5% of Wikipedia's as written;
  ESPDIC would add 2.1% and 2.0% (decision "ESPDIC and WikDict": listed once hosted). Missing are English words in
  the news (of, the, in), units (km, mm, kg) and derived words (grandurbo, akademiuloj, pliigita). Text without
  hats whose bare spelling is a word shows that word first (au before aŭ, si before ŝi, logantoj before loĝantoj),
  the hat word after it. The x-system reads foreign words too (aux, Bordeaux: aŭ, Bordeaŭ), which find an entry
  only where one exists; the h-system is not read. Czech and Slovak names in OCR text take the circumflex (Ŝkoda,
  Ĉapek), and ǔ typed in place of ŭ (aǔ, laǔ: 73 times in 200,000 sentences) is fixed only in OCR text. A spaced
  "k. t. p." and "a. K." before a capital keep the sentence running (p. holds before anything; K. reads as an
  initial). An elided o joined to the next word (dank'al, 42 times) is not split.
- Welsh: a word that a mutation rule turns into a headword finds that word and not the marked one the writer meant
  without the circumflex: dwr finds twr (heap; cy-en also has dwr as its soft form), not dŵr; ddwr finds dŵr. A
  listed word that is a mutated form in some text (dal "still", also the soft form of tal) is found as that form
  only through the dictionary's own row. cy-en has wrong rows from mutation tables of mutated forms (dal → thal,
  nhal) and shows them under the rule's name. Mutated words the dictionary lacks find the radical through the rule
  whatever the context (lan finds glan, which is right after ar). The OCR variant needs the overlay's engine: a word
  read by OCR but copied into the search screen is looked up as typed. The French edition's mutation rows also
  name forms of other words as the lemma: nghath → cathod ("nasalization"), so nghath shows cathod before cath;
  1,488 of cy-fr's 3,875 mutation rows and 727 of cy-en's 20,532 do not end like their lemma, some of them right
  (jip → tsip, risiau → gris), so the rows are kept.
- Irish: the rules undo every word that starts like a mutated one, whatever the context: words the list does not
  hold also find the radical, mostly right (bhí finds bí, i ndiaidh finds diaidh), sometimes beside an entry of their
  own (12.0% of ga-en tokens show an extra entry). The list holds only the words the review found wrong among the
  1,000 commonest. ts → s after the article is named lenition, as Wiktionary's rows name it (an tSín), not
  t-prothesis. The Tironian et (⁊ for agus) is a symbol and is not looked up as agus. Words are looked up with the
  fada as written (question 11), so a fada the recognizer drops loses the word: ML Kit read hÉireann as hEireann on
  the test screen, which finds nothing (cloud recognition read it). Without a frequency dictionary a word reached
  through two dictionary rows keeps the dictionaries' order: Chuaigh shows cuach (its genitive singular definite
  row) before téigh (past indicative).
- Scottish Gaelic: the rules undo every word that starts like a mutated one, as in Irish (5.16% of gd-en tokens show
  an extra entry after the list). A prefix without its hyphen (hAlba, as some older texts write it) is not undone;
  h-, t- and n- are written with the hyphen in the current spelling. A word without the grave that a mutation rule
  turns into a headword finds that word and not the marked one (as dwr in Welsh). The acute is read as the grave in
  any text, so an acute that recognition gives for a grave finds the word as well; the older spelling's é and ó for
  short vowels (cóig for còig) are also covered.

## Owner decisions in this review

Words hyphenated at a line end (owner: "try to join, since it may not be one word"): the lookup tries the joined
word and the word with its hyphen, and keeps the text as written when neither is found.

Furigana markers outside Japanese (the plan's decision row "Furigana markers"):

- The names stay Yomitan's (`{furigana}`, `{furigana-plain}`, `{sentence-furigana}`, `{sentence-furigana-plain}`).
- They are offered only for Japanese, Chinese and Russian: languages whose readings go over or into the words. Korean
  is left out (hanja are rare in modern text). A field that already has them keeps getting them in any language.
- Chinese and Russian show what the markers give as a second line under each name in the + menu.
- Russian: the stress goes into the text. `{furigana}` and `{furigana-plain}` give ко́шка; in a sentence only words
  written in their dictionary form get stress marks, since an inflected form may stress another syllable.
  Before: `<ruby>кошка<rt>ко́шка</rt></ruby>`, `кошка[ко́шка]`, and the lemma's stress over an inflected word.

Thai sentence ends and starts inside a word (group 4c plan, questions 10-12; owner: "проверь как адекватнее будет, в
каком варианте меньше неправильных определений границ предложений"), measured 2026-10-20 with the rule's own code
(ICU4J 66.1) on text with known boundaries; every current choice had the fewest errors, so none changed:

- Sentences: ORCHID (9,985 paragraphs, sentences joined with a space, 13,140 boundaries) and the Thai side of the
  Asian Language Treebank (1,888 Wikinews articles, one translated sentence per line, joined with a space, 18,218
  boundaries). A space after ๆ before a Thai letter is a boundary 202 times and inside a sentence 1,431 times in
  ORCHID (ALT: 116 and 1,255); a space next to a ฯ outside ฯลฯ 29 and 248 times (ALT: 24 and 228). Wrong cuts plus
  wrong merges: any ฯ holds and ๆ holds (now) 19,396 (ALT 33,672); only ฯลฯ holds 19,615 (33,876); a space after ๆ
  ends 20,625 (34,811). Most errors of every variant are spaces inside a sentence, which the decided rule takes for
  sentence ends.
- Starts inside a word: every letter of every word of 4,000 Khmer sentences of the Asian Language Treebank (316,124
  aims; gold words and compounds from its segmentation, wty km-en and km-ru as the dictionary) and of 3,000 ORCHID
  paragraphs (312,970 aims; its words, wty th-en). The first word shown was the gold word under the aimed letter:
  inside starts only when nothing covers the letter (now) 78.6% Khmer, 60.0% Thai; never 70.9%, 59.8%; always,
  their words first, 59.1%, 31.7% (always, their words after the others, shows the same first word as now). The
  first word covered the aimed letter: 95.1% / 98.5% now, 84.6% / 95.3% never. A gold word was in the dictionary at
  89.6% of the Khmer aims and 70.3% of the Thai ones.

Persian spoken endings (group 4d overnight question 4; owner: "Measure on real text … how often each ending brings
a wrong entry above the right one. Keep only the endings that help more often than they mislead"), measured
2026-10-20. Each word of wordfreq's `small_fa` list (30,182 Persian words; Wikipedia, subtitles, web, Twitter) was
looked up alone with the Persian bundle with and without the ending, ordered as `candidate_lookup.cpp` orders
results (match length, steps, shortest chain, the word itself, wordfreq rank), against wty fa-en's 14,786 headwords
and 105,442 form rows. The words whose first result changes among the 20,000 commonest were judged by hand (by the
agent, not a Persian speaker): R, the word is the stem with this spoken ending and the first entry is the stem; D, the
entry is the word's base, but the ending is named wrong (adverbs in -اً, Arabic plurals in -ین, adjectives in -انه);
N, neither the entry before nor after is right; W, a wrong entry or the ending named on a word not formed so (a name,
a loan, a past participle, a word of its own). Shares are of the tokens of those 20,000 words.

| Ending | R | D | N | W | Kept |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| copula -ه | 224 words, 0.374% | 73, 0.133% | 12, 0.015% | 170, 0.835% | no |
| copula, stems in د/ت skipped | 197, 0.323% | 69, 0.122% | 8 | 128, 0.395% | no |
| object -و | 40, 0.064% | — | 1 | 63, 0.082% | no |
| object -رو | 7, 0.021% | — | — | 13, 0.018% | no (a tie within the judging error) |
| plural -ا | 40, 0.054% | 46, 0.080% | 7, 0.028% | 101, 0.062% | no |
| -مون | 30, 0.045% | — | — | 10, 0.008% | yes |
| -تون | 37, 0.059% | — | — | 17, 0.019% | yes |
| -شون | 46, 0.066% | — | — | 4, 0.015% | yes |
| -ین (2pl. verbs, ordinals) | 7, 0.041% | 28, 0.016% | 10 | 54, 0.114% | no |
| -مین → nothing | 16, 0.027% | — | — | 4, 0.006% | — |
| -مین → م, after a number (shipped) | 20, 0.033% | — | — | 0 | yes |

The wrong ones: the copula takes past participles and verbs for nouns and ranks the noun above the verb (داره → دار,
کرده → کرد, بوده → بود, اینکه → اینک); the object marker cuts loans and names (فالو, رزرو, پیرو) and برو → بر; the
plural gives the stem of adverbs in -اً (دقیقا → دقیق, named a plural: D) and names (زهرا); -ین takes spoken
2nd-plural verbs (دارین, کردین) and Arabic plurals (مسلمین, معلمین) for ordinals; -مین → nothing gave سومین → سو
and تامین → تا, so the shipped rule leaves the ordinal in -م (سومین → سوم) and runs after a number only. With the
kept rules the first result changes for 164 of the 20,000 words (0.25% of the tokens) and 349 of all 30,182; besides
the judged ones, همه‌شون, همه‌مون, خونه‌مون and همه‌تون now match whole, and استون shows اس above ستون. The plural -ا
named also as the adverb ending -اً would make its D words right (R + D 0.134%, W 0.062%): a question for the owner.

Audio sources without recordings (question 14; owner: "Если 100% вообще нет, то скипаем для таких"): Commons was
counted on 2026-10-20 for every language's Lingua Libre item (`LL-Q…`) and Wiktionary prefix: each has recordings in
both sources; the fewest are Khmer's (13 and 9) and Croatian's own Lingua Libre item (1, besides the 93 of the Serbian
one it searches as well). So no source is left out, and the order stays.

Look-alike rules and app text (owner, 2026-10-20, plan request 69): every built language's rule that fixes or matches letters an engine misreads was checked for the text it reads. The one-for-one fixes in `ocrWord` and `ocrLines` were OCR only already. The Czech capital I of "n. I." moved from `endsSentence` to `ocrLines`, as nobody types it. Arabic text with the Persian ک ی is matched in any text, as Persian keyboards and fonts give them and Arabic never writes them. The other rules that reach app text stay for any text, since people type or paste text that way: Japanese NFKC, radicals and respellings, Chinese radicals, Arabic and Persian spellings, Khmer, Thai, Indic, Armenian, Georgian and Greek spellings, stress marks, ё/е, apostrophes, Serbo-Croatian Latin without diacritics, Vietnamese tone-mark placement, Latin diacritics. The data check of
2026-10-22 (next section) found one exception: Thai sara am written as ํ + า is a Tesseract misreading that typed
text almost never has, so its reading belongs to OCR text only (group 4c plan, question 15, open).

## Thai and Khmer rules checked on data

Each Thai and Khmer rule's premise counted on text with known forms on 2026-10-22 (group 4c plan, "Measurements",
which has the table of numbers): the Thai and Khmer sides of the Asian Language Treebank (ALT: 20,106 lines each, one
translated Wikinews sentence per line, NICT, CC BY-NC-SA), ORCHID (Thai, from vistec-AI/crfcut), the headwords,
glosses and examples of wty th-en and km-en, and the output of every engine on the OCR comparison screens. The
corpora stayed in the session's scratch folder.

- Thai sara am: counted as ํ + า (also with a tone mark between them) against ำ, per source. Typed text has the split
  form 3 times in 17,774 (ALT) and 0 in 23,934 (ORCHID); Tesseract `tha` gives it for every ำ (fast 6 of 6, best 8
  of 8, at times ก | ํ | า across its cluster words, so `ocrWord` cannot fix it one for one); Paddle and cloud never.
- Thai tone mark or thanthakhat before the vowel above: ALT 10 in 68,519, ORCHID 10 in 64,378, wty 0; no engine.
- Thai ๆ: a space before it 442 times in ALT against 1,448 joined (ORCHID 68 and 3,355); wty headwords 43 of 44
  spaced. Both forms are common, so the space is tried both ways for any text.
- Thai period: 16 of the 20,106 ALT lines end with a period after a Thai letter; 1,095 such periods stand inside
  lines (abbreviations, numbers).
- Khmer zero-width spaces: 2,730 ALT lines carry 15,276; wty and the engines none.
- Khmer subscript TA and DA: ALT running text has TA 18,456 times and DA 1,827; 22 phrases are written both ways.
  Tesseract `khm` wrote DA where the ground truth has TA 4-6 times (truth: TA 64, DA 0).
- Khmer sentence ends: 16,675 ALT lines end with ។ and 3,355 with a quote; ។ stands inside a line 3,360 times, mostly
  before a closing quote; ។ល។ (etc.) twice; spaces inside lines 78,934, which end no sentence.
- Khmer form-of rows: km-en's tags are abstract-noun (2,795), alternative (795) and alt-of (310); no classifier rows.
- Shorter matches (only those that end at an ICU word boundary): every match from every start of the ALT Khmer
  sentences, judged against the ALT segmentation with its bracketed compounds (ICU4J 66.1, wty km-en and km-ru
  headwords, TA read as DA). A match is real when it is a gold word or compound or a dictionary phrase made of whole
  gold words, otherwise a fragment. The real ones hidden lie under a longer gold word or compound (67%), under a set
  phrase of whole words (32%: នៅក្នុង, គឺជា, មិនបាន, ធ្វើឲ្យ) or under a wrong longer word (1%, 792: ការ under ការបរ,
  ខែ under ខែក, ពី under ពីរ). Most often hidden (behind a longer fragment): នៅ, មាន, ការ, ធ្វើ, មិន. ORCHID has
  no compound units, so its Thai shares count words under compounds as lost.
- Space gap: the gaps between Tesseract's words on the comparison screens against the ground truth's spaces, per
  share of the line height (Thai 0.4: 6 of 15 spaces missed and none added on the clean screens; Khmer 0.15: 5 of 19
  missed and 1 added on the hard ones). An added space breaks a word, a missed one only lengthens the text looked up,
  so the gap stays where the added ones are fewest.

## Kazakh and Kyrgyz rules checked on data

Each Kazakh and Kyrgyz rule's premise counted on 2026-10-22 (group 4e plan, "Measurements", which has the table of
numbers): Leipzig's news samples (`kaz_newscrawl_2016_100K`, `kir_newscrawl_2016_100K`: 100,000 sentences each,
1,079,880 and 1,317,643 Cyrillic words; the 10K samples for the hyphens), the headwords and tags of wty kk and ky into
English and Russian (releases 2026.10.11 and 2026.10.22), the app's bundles (`kk.js`, `ky.js`) run on the 30,000 most
frequent word forms of each sample (981,616 and 1,223,872 occurrences), and the output of every engine on the OCR
comparison screens. The corpora stayed in the session's scratch folder.

- Ending rules: of the top forms by occurrence, Kazakh 65.2% are headwords as written, 27.9% are found through the
  endings, 6.8% by nothing (before the fixes 41-43: 27.1% and 7.6%; on wty 2026.10.22 65.3% as written: ҚР and ҚФФ
  became headwords, Венгрия lost its only row); Kyrgyz 42.0%, 23.1%, 34.9% on both releases. What is left is mostly
  missing from the dictionaries: Kazakh пен, бен (and, with), the particles та, те, па, пе, ме written apart, names;
  Kyrgyz менен 16,803, боюнча 7,885, бирок 4,522, тууралуу, чейин, же. Kyrgyz verbal nouns in -ыш with a possessive
  have no rule (болушу 599, калышы, кетиши, билдиришинче: 144 forms, 4,235 occurrences, less келишим "agreement",
  which is a word of its own); see "Limits left as they are".
- Verb chains keep only the definitions tagged `v`: the filter removes every verb reading of 507 Kazakh forms (30,227
  occurrences: бар as бау, бір as бу, қарсы as қау) and 903 Kyrgyz ones (61,810: жана as жоо, эле as элүү, көп as
  көө). It leaves nothing for 124 Kazakh forms (1,330) and 497 Kyrgyz ones (13,844). Of the 25 most frequent of
  those, the Kazakh ones are 14 verb forms (282 occurrences: өзгертіп, күшейтіп, шалып) of verbs wty gives only
  as a noun or adjective (өзгерту, күшейту `n`) and 11 wrong readings (529: сай as сау); the Kyrgyz ones 9 verb forms
  (1,634: жашаган, жашап, сурап, чакырды, тосуп) of verbs wty gives only as a verbal noun (жашоо, суроо, чакыруу
  `n`) and 16 wrong readings (5,428: азыр as азуу, булар as буу). Of the verb-shaped headwords, Kazakh 515 of 4,200
  (-у) and Kyrgyz 213 of 622 (-уу -оо -өө -үү) carry no `v` row, mostly real nouns and adjectives (ауру, сулуу).
- Letter keys: OCR loses the Kazakh letters only on busy screens and only with Paddle (31 of 458 lost to their Russian
  twins on the hard screens: қ 12, ң 11, і 5; 0 on the clean ones); Tesseract lost 1, cloud none. In typed text the
  keyed lookup finds 48 Kazakh forms (424 occurrences: Ирак for Ирақ, кенесары) and 5 Kyrgyz ones (51); ы matched to
  і would raise the shared keys from 0.9% to 1.5% (question 12).
- Look-alikes: Paddle wrote Latin letters inside 7 Cyrillic words of 678 (жанa 3 times, тyман, тумаH, сааm),
  5 of which the shared Cyrillic table fixes; m for т is not in it. Tesseract and cloud wrote none. Kazakh text
  writes Latin i, I, h as 39%, 44%, 60% of і, І, һ and ə as 0.06% of ә; Kyrgyz ɵ is 0.04% of ө.
- Hyphens (10K samples, group 4e question 14): Kazakh parts after a word 1,867, a suffix entry for the whole part
  486 times (-ақ 145, -де 127), only a shorter one 980; Kyrgyz 2,119, 17 whole, 1,334 shorter; after digits Kazakh
  287 whole, 102 shorter, Kyrgyz 0 and 619 (19-декабрь → -де).
- Sentence ends: an abbreviation of the lists before a lowercase word or a digit inside a Leipzig sentence (held
  together, as Leipzig has it): Kazakh 81 (т.б. 51, ж. 18), Kyrgyz 115 (млн 65, млрд 40, б.а. 6); before a capital
  inside one (the rule ends the sentence): Kazakh 74, all right (67 dateline years, "2012 ж. Ақтөбеде", and 7 real
  ends after т.б. and т.с.с. that Leipzig missed), Kyrgyz 2 lowercase ones. A capital initial with a space before a
  surname ended the sentence in every spaced language: Kyrgyz 891 times (К. Бакиев, Ж. Оторбаев), Kazakh 83 (its news
  writes Қ.Мәсімов without a space, which ends nothing); now it holds (question 19).
- Noun endings on pronouns (question 16) and PanLex's capitalized headwords (question 18) are counted in the plan.
- After the pronoun list (question 16), on the 30,000 most frequent forms with wty 2026.10.22 en and ru and the
  engine's order: the first reading comes through noun endings for 4,810 Kyrgyz forms (151,615 occurrences) and 9,096
  Kazakh ones (173,245). Reviewed by eye (the most frequent with a verb, form or noun alternative, the most frequent
  alone, 40 at random from rank 201 on): Kyrgyz 48 of 252 wrong (13,162 of 53,374 occurrences), Kazakh 39 of 254
  (6,311 of 44,646); at random Kyrgyz 4 of 40, Kazakh 1 of 40. Causes and counts are in the plan.
- The Kyrgyz -ыш rule (question 21) reads 599 of the 30,000 forms (15,486 occurrences): a new first reading for 351
  (8,683), a reading after a noun for 146 (4,396), the same first reading for 102 (the past plural -ышты, 2,407).
  Reviewed 448 (14,779 occurrences): 39 wrong first readings (about 1,271), all nouns wty lacks (келишим family 757,
  талаш 208, жарыш 75, жылыш 69, жетишсиз 57, катышы 53), and the жумуш family (20 forms, 620) with жумуу after it.
  Nouns in -ш with an entry (иш, киши, тиш, күмүш, баш) reach no verb tagged `v`, so they keep their reading.

## Group 5a rules checked on data

Each rule's premise counted on typed text and on the engines' output, per language as it is built (group 5a plan,
"Measurements", has the tables). Typed text: Leipzig sentence lists (news 2022 and Wikipedia 2021, 100,000 sentences
each, one sentence per line, CC BY); dictionaries: wty <lang>-en headwords of release 2026.10.11 (counts on
2026.10.22 under "Recheck on wty 2026.10.22"); recognized text: the 5a OCR screens
(`testdata/ocr/compare/new/5a`). The corpora stayed in the session's scratch folder.

### Swedish (2026-10-22)

- Abbreviations: each counted inside a sentence before a number, a lowercase word or a capital, and at a sentence's
  end. Leipzig's splitter cuts after some of them (lines ending "som t.ex.", "ledamot nr."), so an end counted for an
  abbreviation that never ends a sentence is the corpus's cut. t.ex. 46 times before a lowercase word, 11 before a
  name; bl.a. 67 and 16; kl. 22 before a number. st. stands before a name 51 times (St. Eriks, St. Louis) and ends a
  sentence 5 times (the newspaper ST), so it moved to the abbreviations that end nothing. ung. never came before a
  number; its 3 cases before a capital are the adjective ending a sentence ("ung. Hon ville"). f.Kr. 20 times before a
  lowercase word, 7 before a capital that starts a sentence. osv., m.m. and etc. come before a lowercase word 7 times
  and before a capital or at the end 32 times, so they moved to the before-lowercase list.
- Ordinals: "5. maj" style 23 times, "1:a" 476 times; 6,965 sentences end with a number and a period. No ordinal
  period holds.
- The OCR letter fix: typed text has no ả; â 422 times, all in names (Ouâdi, Râs, Château); à 80 times, 32 of them
  the price word à, so à stays as read. ML Kit wrote ả for å twice on the clean screens and nothing else; on the hard
  screens it read å as ä twice and as à once (ä is a Swedish letter, so those stay wrong). No other engine wrote ả,
  â or à.
- Quotes: ” 18,818 times in 9,731 sentences, “ 108, » 11, " 10,889; of 8,432 spans in ”…”, 10 hold a sentence end
  followed by more quoted text, the case the missing pair affects.
- Compounds: lowercase words of six or more letters without a sv-en headword (59,867 kinds, 85,875 times): 76% of the
  kinds split; of the 1,000 most frequent, 709. A random 40: 35 right; wrong are a form taking the link letter (jobbs
  + kapare, fisket + rister, both the longer shortest part), a word missing from the dictionary (borrelia, företagar)
  and a typo. Most frequent wrong: renovering as ren + ove + ring, manager as mana + ger.

### Norwegian (2026-10-22)

Leipzig's Bokmål news (2019) and Norwegian Wikipedia (2021), 100,000 sentences each; nb-en as the dictionary.

- Abbreviations: f.eks. 51 times before a lowercase word, 17 before a capital (mostly names: f.eks. Sarpsborg); bl.a.
  71 and 23; ca. 613 before a number; nr. 119; kl. 25. Leipzig's splitter cuts after kl. and ca. (126 and 105 lines
  end there), so those ends are its cuts. st. stands before a capital 217 times (St. Petersburg) and ends a sentence
  29 times, so it ends nothing. s. comes before a number 610 times and before a capital 47 (initials: S. Bach); f.Kr.
  102 times before a lowercase word, 28 before a capital; osv. 3 before a lowercase word and 27 before a capital or at
  the end; m.fl. and mfl. join the before-lowercase list as in Swedish.
- Ordinals: "5. word" 7,079 times inside sentences; a one- or two-digit number and a period end 878 sentences and stand
  before a capital 430 times. The ordinal period keeps those 878 running into the next sentence, the smaller error.
- The OCR letter fix: typed ả once (Vietnamese Giải), â 40 times and à 85 (foreign names, the price word à 29), ä 713
  (Lagerbäck 24, Märtha 18, Swedish names and words). ML Kit wrote ả for å once on the clean screens, ä and â once each
  on the hard ones, o for ø once; no other engine wrote a look-alike for å.
- Quotes: « 11,586 and » 11,337 times in about 9,700 sentences; ” 329, “ 94, " 2,901. A quote is followed by a
  lowercase word in 4,178 sentences («Kom», sa hun.).
- The genitive -s (dagens → dagen), in nb-en and the Leipzig text (2,587,822 lowercase tokens). Without a type, the
  rule finds 13,659 words that have no entry of their own and adds 25,449 extra entries to words that have one (911
  kinds: hos → ho "she", mens → men "but", ellers → eller). The result restricted to nouns by Yomitan's recursion
  finds 13,127 with 10,801 extra (166 kinds). By the definition tags, wty's only part-of-speech field: noun and
  form tags find 13,500 (2,367 kinds) with 12,311 extra (250 kinds, mostly mens > men "harm", hos > ho, finnes >
  finne); adding verbs finds 13,971 with 20,533 extra; nouns alone find 3,025 (forms like dagen are tagged non-lemma).
  Capitalized words: noun and form tags find 3,648, adding the name tag 6,518 (Norges 718, Bergens 101, Europas 80)
  with 938 extra. Taken: noun, name and form tags.
- Compounds: lowercase words of six or more letters without a nb-en headword (86,486 kinds, 170,444 times): 66.7% of
  the kinds split; of the 1,000 most frequent, 591. A random 40: 32 right; wrong are short parts the dictionary has
  (kollet = kol + let, krenkende = krenk + ende, premieringer = premie + ringer, takstoler = takst + oler, taurus,
  forbitrede, departementalt) and a genitive ending taken as a part (vognmandsforretning + ens). Before the genitive rule the most frequent splits were genitives (dagens = dag + ens,
  verdens, kongens); the genitive now finds them first.

### Danish (2026-10-22)

Leipzig's Danish news (2022) and Wikipedia (2021), 100,000 sentences each; da-en as the dictionary.

- Abbreviations: f.eks. 387 times before a lowercase word, 51 before a capital (names: f.eks. New York); bl.a. 692

  the splitter's cuts. s. comes before a number 446 times and before a capital 57 (initials: S. Hansen); f.Kr. 33
  lowercase, 5 capital; osv. 13 lowercase, 67 capital or end; mv. 13 and 28; m.m. 5 and 28; so osv., mv., m.v., m.m.,
  etc. and o.l. join the before-lowercase list, as in Swedish and Norwegian.
- Ordinals: "5. word" 7,584 times inside sentences; a one- or two-digit number and a period end 1,140 sentences and
  stand before a capital 662 times. The ordinal period keeps those 1,140 running into the next sentence.
- The OCR letter fix: typed ả never, â 47 and à 51 (foreign names, the price word à 21), ä 447 (Händel, Barsebäck,
  Swedish län). ML Kit wrote ả for å 3 times on the clean screens (and once for nothing), ä 3 and â 2 times on the hard
  ones; it also read ø as o 4 times and æ as e once, which no one-for-one fix can undo; no other engine misread æ, ø
  or å.
- Quotes: " 17,511 times, » 3,023 and « 2,917, ” 2,432, “ 557, „ 30. Spans: »…« 1,874, «…» 228, “…” 402, „…“ 24;
  ” opens a quote 907 times (”…”, which pairs nothing, as in Swedish). Paired: »…« and “…”.
- Compounds: lowercase words of six or more letters without a da-en headword (87,650 kinds, 220,420 times): 63.5% of
  the kinds split; of the 1,000 most frequent, 499. A random 40 of all: 33 right; a random 40 of the most frequent: 29
  right. da-en (24k lemmas) lacks common words (finale, serie, situation, indeholde, basere, version), whose forms
  then split into nonsense (finalen = fin + alen, modellen = mod + ellen, baseret = base + ret); the dictionary's
  form rows cover the inflection of the words it has (huset, bilerne, dagens).

### Romanian (2026-10-22)

Leipzig's Romanian news (2022) and Wikipedia (2021), 100,000 sentences each; ro-en as the dictionary; wordfreq's
small Romanian list for coverage.

- Letters: ș 163,721 and ț 164,846 times against the cedilla letters ş 22,478 and ţ 25,413 (about one in eight); Ș
  3,410, Ş 647. 9,554 of 165,603 longer sentences carry no Romanian mark at all.
- Abbreviations inside sentences: p. before a digit 577; nr. always before a number; dr. 19 and prof. 68 before a
  lowercase word; sec. 32 lowercase, 17 before a capital (Roman numerals: sec. XIV); ed. 33 before a capital (Ed. =
  Editura); etc. 27 lowercase, 65 capital; î.Hr. 28 lowercase, 14 capital; gen. 6 capital (Gen. Ion), 3 lowercase;
  n. 79 before a digit (născut); v. 64 before a capital (initials), 11 lowercase. The ends counted for nr (806), dr
  (338) and sf (332) are the splitter's cuts. loc, sat, lei, mii, com and or end sentences as words ("a avut loc.")
  and are not abbreviations.
- Numbers: a one- or two-digit number with a period stands before a lowercase word 15 times, before a capital 403
  times, and ends 1,257 sentences: no ordinal period (ordinals are written al 5-lea, 611 times).
- Quotes: spans „…” 10,976, “…” 1,033, „…“ 407, «…» 346, »…« 54; ” opens a quote 2,148 times; " 13,347. Paired: „…”,
  “…” and «…».
- Apostrophes: 902, mostly in English names; Ukrainian's apostrophe processor covers the few Romanian ones (dom'le).
- Hyphens, the first part and its count: s 13,128, într 5,560, i 2,847, să 2,720, și 2,674 (şi 366), l 2,545, de
  1,737, a 1,290, le 1,233, dintr 875, n 749, ne 670, printr 629, nu 629, mi 540, m 510, ce 220, intr 213, v 193, si
  162, te 132, ți 80. The part after the hyphen is a headword in nearly every case; whole tokens that are headwords:
  i-a 2,620, m-am 419, într-adevăr 136. Pronouns and prepositions before the hyphen start a new word after it (the
  plan's list with mi, ți, și, le, te and the spellings without marks or with a cedilla); să, nu, ce and a are
  followed by an enclitic pronoun (să-l, nu-i), which stays with them. Enclitic tokens: 18,859; the part before the
  hyphen is found for 11,885.
- wty ro-en: form rows are written without marks, the spelling in the reading (["numarul", "numărul", "non-lemma"]);
  lemma rows keep theirs (43,324 with a mark). The plan's first count (1,215 of the top 5,000 missing) took the
  stripped forms for missing; with the text also looked up without marks, 395 of the top 5,000 are missing.
- Coverage on wordfreq's top 30,000: 24,770 found, 5,230 not. The chosen endings reach 1,086 of the 5,230 (126 of
  the 395 in the top 5,000). Without the entry filter they would add another lemma to 3,371 found words (casă →
  cas), about one word in seven. "-a → ă" and the verb endings (există → exista) reached none, as wty lists verb
  forms; the subjunctive endings gave more wrong lemmas than right ones: all three were left out.
- Keys without marks: 34 words of the top 30,000 are found only through them (cand, dupa, asa, il, fara); 832
  frequent words share a key with another word (si → și, sa → să) and show the exact spelling first.
- Cyrillic: 217 form-of items link Latin words to Moldovan Cyrillic spellings (о, есте, ши, космонаут; tags
  `Cyrillic` and `alt-of`); 82 lemma rows are Cyrillic, mostly letters. Results under a Cyrillic headword are left
  out. wty 2026.10.22 turned the links around (Cyrillic → Latin), so the filter only matters for older installed
  dictionaries.
- Forms without marks join words that differ by their marks: the form row casa names casă (definite), the verb casa
  (perfect, reading casă) and cașă (reading cașa), so casa finds all three; the form row's reading is not compared
  with the text (a limit, below).

### Latin (2026-10-22)

Leipzig's Latin Wikipedia (2021, 100,000 sentences); classical texts from the Latin Library (Caesar, Gallic War 1-3;
Cicero, Against Catiline 1-2; Livy 1; Ovid, Metamorphoses 1; Tacitus, Annals 1; Vergil, Aeneid 1-2: 65,242 words);
la-en (wty 2026.10.11) as the dictionary.

- wty la-en: 1,977,692 rows, 1,098,991 headwords (57,370 lemma, 1,087,724 form headwords); 6 rows have a macron or
  breve in the headword. Form rows name their lemma with marks (amat → amō): of 2,164,872 lemma names, 63.7% resolve
  as written, 99.7% without macrons and breves. The rules field carries parts of speech (v 1.52M rows, adj 207k, n
  183k, none 61k, adv 4k); form readings carry macrons.
- Coverage of lowercase words: Wikipedia 842,700 words, 94.8% found as written, 95.0% with Yomitan's processors; the
  enclitics reach 0.56% more, Yomitan's rules 0.04%; 4.4% stay missing (English and German words, abbreviations: of,
  and, the, cf, al, km). Classical texts 58,985 words (v spelling): 96.8% and 97.0%; the enclitics 2.71% more, the
  rules 0.01%; 0.3% missing.
- Enclitics where the word has no entry: Wikipedia 4,757 tokens (que 4,053, ve 630, ne 74), classical 1,601; of a
  random 40 each, 39 and 40 right (assertive → asserti + ve the wrong one). Where the word has an entry they would
  add another: Wikipedia 11,674 tokens (atque → at 2,038, sive → si 1,127, quoque → quo 1,055, sine → si 669,
  quinque → quin 399, neque → ne 363), classical 1,123. They apply only where the text has no entry.
- Yomitan's rules on words with an entry of their own: another lemma in 5,662 of 107,680 result tokens on Wikipedia
  (die → di 867, si → sus 747, re → R 541, gratia → gratius 407), 611 of 7,790 on the classical texts; the same
  filter removes them.
- u for v, on the classical texts converted to u spelling (5,036 words with v): one u at a time finds 4,643; 83 need
  two at once (vivere, vivo, conviviis); 183 u forms are headwords of their own (uero, ui) and found as written; 304
  v words are not found anyway. Turning every u at once would be wrong 339 times. In v-spelled Wikipedia text the
  processor adds a second entry after the exact one for 350 of 1.1M tokens (voluit → volvit 140, voluerunt →
  volverunt 37, deseruit → deservit 19).
- Abbreviations (Leipzig's splitter cuts at many of them, so the ends include its cuts): a.C.n. 383 before a
  lowercase word, 37 before a capital; p.C.n. 48 and 6; cf. 102 lowercase, 10 capital; e.g. 156 and 94 (names
  follow); i.e. 90 and 25; before a capital C. 95, M. 93, L. 53, T. 38 (praenomina and initials) and S. 140
  (sanctus); sex. ends 18 sentences (the numeral); m. lowercase is metres (19); p. before a digit 251; etc. 20
  lowercase, none before a capital. M'. (Manius) does not occur.
- Numbers with a period: before a lowercase word 82, before a capital 201, ending a sentence 276: no ordinal period.
- Quotes: " 13,965; « 780 and » 787; “ 348, ” 268, „ 92. Spans: »…« 665 (mostly titles, German style), «…» 350, “…”
  236, „…“ 76; a sentence end inside a span 4-5 times. Paired: “…” and «…».
- Letters in Wikipedia: ā 555, ă 191, æ 198, j 1,452, J 1,547; the Latin Library writes no j.
- Audio: 745 Latin Wiktionary recordings on Commons: La-cls- 624, La-ecc- 81, La-rom- 16, La-pl- 4, 20 untagged;
  Lingua Libre Q397 has 213. Transcription tags in the IPA file: Classical-Latin 84,146, ⛪ 79,636, Ecclesiastical 5,411.
- Fonts: Atkinson Hyperlegible Next lacks ō Ō (and ȳ and the breves); PT Sans and PT Serif lack ȳ Ȳ and the breves;
  Literata, Inter and Lexend have all.

- Fonts: every catalog Latin font has ą č ę ė į š ų ū ž.

### Recheck on wty 2026.10.22

wty 2026.10.22 reverses sense-level alt-of rows, gives misspelling, nonstandard, obsolete and pronunciation-spelling
senses form rows, keeps the glosses of abbreviation senses and flips other-script rows. The counts above came from
2026.10.11; run again on 2026.10.22 with the same scripts and corpora (group 5a plan, "Recheck on wty 2026.10.22",
has the table), they moved by under one point and every rule holds:

- Swedish compounds: 59,840 kinds without a headword, 76.4% split, 716 of the 1,000 most frequent.
- Norwegian: the genitive by noun, name and form tags finds 13,492 lowercase words (11,850 extra) and 6,754
  capitalized ones (USAs and NRKs are new); with verbs 13,972 and 20,533; compounds 86,480 kinds, 67.2%, 601.
- Danish compounds: 87,641 kinds, 63.5%, 499.
- Romanian: 5,141 of the top 30,000 not found, the endings reach 1,086; 35 words found only through the keys without
  marks, 833 sharing a key. The joined forms of the hyphen rule have entries again (s-a "contraction of se and a",
  l-a, într-o): aimed at s the lookup reaches s-a, aimed at a it still looks up a (checked on the emulator). No
  Latin word links to a Cyrillic spelling any more; 1,615 `alternative` and 79 `Cyrillic` rows link the Cyrillic
  spelling to the Latin one (кат → cat), which Latin text does not reach. `showsHeadword` stays for dictionaries
  installed from older releases.
- Latin: 63.2% of 2,164,636 lemma names resolve as written, 99.9% without marks; the enclitics reach 4,758 tokens, u
  for v finds 4,646 of 5,036.
- Lithuanian: lemma names 71.2% as written, 71.9% without stress (71.8% keeping the dot); 245,024 words with the
  letters' marks, 47,631 with a bare spelling that is a word.

Abbreviations and contractions have entries again (da f.eks., kl.; ro nr., str.; la C., M.; lt pvz., tūkst.); the
sentence rules read text only, so they do not change. Rows the reversal removed were extra results under common words
(sv gå, t.ex.; nb men → mein; la atque → ac; lt į → i̷); new obsolete rows (la amo → hama) are left as the data gives
them.
